TITLE: Boat Rental Marbella 2026: Yachts, Catamarans &amp; Charters
DESCRIPTION: Boat rental in Marbella from €749 for 2h on our 12.5 m flybridge motor yachts (Astondoa 40 + Azimut 39). Departing Puerto Banús. Skipper, fuel, drinks, insurance and VAT included.

We operate a 29-boat fleet in Marbella, led by two 12.5 m flybridge motor yachts — the Astondoa 40 and the Azimut 39 — all departing Puerto Banús with licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, insurance and VAT included. Prices start at €749 for 2 hours and run up to €2,299 for a full 8-hour day . This page is the full overview — fleet, ports, license rules and how to book. Skip the long-form below and message us on WhatsApp for an instant quote with real-time availability across all five ports. Boat rental Marbella prices in 2026 Same boat, same crew, transparent hourly pricing. No high-season uplift, no levante surcharge, no marina fee added at the dock. Duration Price What you get 2 hours €749 Quick spin along the Golden Mile, one swim stop 4 hours €1,299 Half-day — comfortable lunch + swim 6 hours €1,799 Two swim stops, paddleboard, real day out 8 hours €2,299 Full day — Sotogrande or Cabopino itineraries Full grid and per-boat detail at /boats/ . Compare the Astondoa 40 (9 guests, Spanish-built, classic Mediterranean styling) vs the Azimut 39 (11 guests, Italian flybridge, modern lines). Astondoa or Azimut? Both boats are the same length (12.5 m), price (€749 → €2,299), and depart from the same marina (Puerto Banús). What differs: Astondoa 40 — Spanish-built, classic teak and cream interior, up to 9 guests . The "Fufi" you see in our photos. Best for couples and groups up to 8 who want the local Mediterranean feel. Azimut 39 — Italian-built flybridge, modern lines, up to 11 guests . Pick this one for larger groups (9+) or anyone who wants the upper sun-deck experience. Departure ports: Puerto Banús, Marbella Marina, Cabopino, Estepona, Sotogrande Puerto Banús is the default departure for any boat over 12 metres and for everything that calls itself a luxury yacht. It sits 7 km west of Marbella old town, has the deepest berths, and puts the lined-up Ferraris on display as you walk to the pontoon. Marbella Marina (officially Puerto Deportivo Virgen del Carmen) is far more central, walking distance from any old-town hotel, and ideal for guests with mobility constraints. Cabopino, 12 km east, is the quietest pickup point and the closest to the dune beaches; licence-free rentals are concentrated here. Estepona and Sotogrande add another 30 minutes by car each way and are usually only worth it if you're staying there. Our full Puerto Banús vs Marbella Marina comparison covers parking, walking distance and which boats live where. Licence rules you actually need to know You do not need a Spanish boat licence to rent in Marbella as long as either (a) the boat is small enough to fall under the licence-free rules, or (b) the charter comes with a qualified skipper — the case for ~95% of bookings on this site. The licence-free thresholds in Spain are: hull up to 5 metres, motor up to 15 hp, sail up to 6 metres, distance from coast up to 2 nautical miles, daylight hours only, captain at least 18 years old. A quick test before you self-drive: are you confident reversing into a windy berth at Cabopino with a queue of boats waiting? If not, book with a skipper. Full rule details: see our Spain boat licence rules 2026 guide, including what changes if you hold a UK ICC or RYA Day Skipper certificate (you can charter larger boats without a Spanish licence — bring the original certificate, not a copy). What's included — and what is not Standard inclusions on a Marbella skippered charter are the captain, fuel for a normal coastal cruising itinerary, soft drinks and water, ice, light snacks, towels, snorkelling masks and an inflatable donut or paddleboard. What's not included by default: alcohol on board (you can BYO on most boats, ask first), prepared lunch (€25–€60 per head depending on caterer), berth taxes for stops in non-home ports, and tip for the skipper (€20–€50 is normal for a good day, or 10% on luxury charters). Typical itineraries from Marbella Half-day from Puerto Banús: head west past Estepona, stop for a swim at Cala del Faro, optional tapas at Sotogrande, back by sunset. Full-day from Marbella Marina: east towards Cabopino's dunes and snorkel anchorage at Cala Cortés, lunch on board, then ice cream stop in the Cabopino port itself. Two-hour sunset: cruise the Golden Mile coastline at displacement speed, anchor briefly off Nikki Beach, return as the sun drops behind La Concha. A Gibraltar day trip (see our Gibraltar by boat piece) is doable from Sotogrande but adds 80 NM round trip and only makes sense in flat sea. How to book in 60 seconds Three ways: WhatsApp us with your date, group size and rough budget — instant quotes from local operators, no account, no deposit until you confirm. Call if you want to talk through itinerary options first. Browse our 29-boat fleet at /boats/ if you'd rather pick a specific hull yourself. Whichever route you pick, the price you see is what you pay — no booking fees added at checkout, no surprise marina charges. Same advice applies whether you're after a luxury yacht rental Marbella for a corporate day out or a quick 2-hour spin from the central Puerto Banús pontoon. Your browser doesn't support HTML5 video. Follow @boatrentalmarbella Frequently asked questions How much does it cost to rent a boat in Marbella? Boat rental in Marbella on our fleet starts at €749 for 2 hours and runs to €2,299 for a full 8-hour day. Same price across seasons. Every charter includes licensed skipper, fuel, drinks, insurance and Spanish IVA (VAT). For larger groups (9+ guests on Azimut 39) the per-person cost drops to about €70 for the 2-hour cruise. Where do boats depart from in Marbella? The five working pickup points are Puerto Banús (the marquee yacht marina, west of Marbella town), Marbella Marina/Puerto Deportivo Virgen del Carmen (right by the central promenade), Cabopino (eastern Marbella, near the dunes), Estepona, and Sotogrande further west. Puerto Banús handles the largest yachts and most luxury bookings. Do I need a licence to rent a boat in Marbella? No. Spain allows licence-free rentals for boats up to 5 m hull length with engines up to 15 hp (or sail up to 6 m), within 2 nautical miles of the coast, in daylight, captain aged 18 or over. For anything larger, you either hold a PNB/PER licence or — by far the most common option — book a charter with the skipper included. What is included in the price? Every charter on our Astondoa 40 or Azimut 39 includes: licensed Spanish skipper, fuel for the standard coastal route, soft drinks and water, snorkel gear, inflatable donut, paddleboard, towels, insurance and Spanish IVA (VAT). Catered lunch (€25–€60 per head) is the main optional add-on. When is the best time to rent a boat in Marbella? June and September are the sweet spot — warm sea (22–24 °C), long sunshine hours, fewer afternoon levante winds than July–August, and prices roughly 10–15% under peak. July and August are busiest and warmest but choppier from 14:00 onwards. April–May and October still deliver flat morning seas at noticeably lower rates. How far in advance should I book? For July, August and any Saturday in summer, book 2–4 weeks ahead — the larger yachts (15 m+) sell out first. For weekday trips in May, June, September or October, 3–5 days is usually enough. For licence-free boats and weekday 2-hour sunset cruises, same-day WhatsApp bookings are routinely possible. Can children come on the boat? Yes — virtually every operator welcomes children. Home › Astondoa 40 vs Fairline Targa 12M: Marbella Charter Showdown Choosing between the Astondoa 40 and the Fairline Targa 12M for your Marbella charter? Both are top-tier sport cruisers departing from Puerto Banús, but they cater to slightly different needs. The Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 pax) offers all-inclusive Tier A pricing at €749 for 2 hours, while the Fairline Targa 12M (12m, 10 pax) requires a custom quote via WhatsApp. This comparison breaks down dimensions, capacity, pricing, and onboard experience to help you decide which boat suits your group best. Dimensions and Capacity The Astondoa 40 measures 12.5 metres in length and carries up to 9 passengers. The Fairline Targa 12M is slightly shorter at 12 metres but can accommodate 10 guests. That one extra seat makes the Fairline ideal for groups of 10, while the Astondoa is perfect for 9 or fewer. Both boats are designed for day trips along the Marbella coastline, from Puerto Banús to Cabopino or Estepona. Pricing: Fixed vs Flexible The Astondoa 40 is part of our Tier A pricing: €749 for 2 hours, €1,149 for 4 hours, €1,649 for 6 hours, and €2,299 for 8 hours. All prices include skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, insurance, and VAT. The Fairline Targa 12M has pricing on request — simply message us on WhatsApp for a custom quote based on your preferred duration and season. This flexibility can work well if you want a bespoke itinerary. Onboard Experience and Layout The Astondoa 40 is a sporty open cruiser with a spacious sunpad aft and a comfortable cockpit. The Fairline Targa 12M offers a more enclosed cabin and a larger cockpit area, making it better for cooler days or longer cruises. Both have ample seating and a bimini top for shade. The Fairline's cabin includes a small galley and berth, while the Astondoa focuses on day-use comfort. Inclusions: What You Get Every charter from Boat Rental Marbella includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and 21% VAT. No hidden extras. For the Astondoa 40, these are baked into the fixed price. For the Fairline Targa 12M, they are included in the custom quote. Best Use Cases Astondoa 40: Ideal for groups of up to 9 who want a straightforward, all-inclusive booking with transparent pricing. Great for a 2-hour sunset cruise or a full-day exploration to Cala del Faro. Fairline Targa 12M: Perfect for groups of 10, or anyone wanting a slightly more enclosed cabin space. Flexible pricing suits those who want a custom duration or itinerary. Local Itineraries Both boats depart from Puerto Banús. Popular routes include cruising past the Golden Mile, anchoring at Cala Cortés for swimming, or heading to Cabopino for lunch. The skipper knows the best spots for snorkelling and sightseeing, including views of La Concha mountain. Which One to Choose? If you value fixed pricing and a sporty open layout, go with the Astondoa 40 . If you need capacity for 10 or prefer a more enclosed cockpit, the Fairline Targa 12M is your match. Both deliver a premium Marbella charter experience. For more options, explore our full fleet or contact us via WhatsApp for a custom quote. Contact us for a custom quote. Can I book either boat for a half-day charter? Yes. The Astondoa 40 offers fixed 2-hour, 4-hour, 6-hour, and 8-hour slots. For the Fairline Targa 12M, you can request any duration via WhatsApp, and we'll tailor a package to your needs. What is included in the charter for both boats? Both charters include a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and Spanish VAT (21%). No hidden fees. Which boat is better for a larger group? The Fairline Targa 12M accommodates 10 passengers, one more than the Astondoa 40's 9. If you have a group of 10, the Fairline is the better choice. For 9 or fewer, either boat works. Where do these boats depart from? Both the Astondoa 40 and Fairline Targa 12M depart from Puerto Banús, the glamorous marina in Marbella. You can also arrange pick-up from Marbella Marina or other nearby spots on request. Are these boats suitable for families with children? Absolutely. Stag and hen specifics Stag and hen weekends in Marbella usually combine the boat party with one of three add-ons: Jet-ski session tied to the charter — skipper coordinates pickup, €60–€90/person. For a quieter group experience that's more day-out than party, the yacht charter Marbella page covers standard private charters. What you can and can't do Yes: BYO alcohol on private charters, loud music at anchor outside marina limits, jumping off the boat at the anchor stop, costume changes on board, inflatable swans, drone photography (your own pilot). No: glass on deck (cans and plastic only on most boats), confetti or anything that ends up in the water (Spanish marina fines are punishing), open flames (no shisha or candles), drugs (the skipper will return to port and the deposit is forfeit). Spanish maritime alcohol law applies to the skipper, not guests — but be sensible. Best months for a Marbella boat party Late June through early September is peak — water at 22–24 °C, long daylight, the marina scene at full volume. July is the busiest single month. Mid-September keeps the warmth and drops the price 10–15%. May, June and October work for groups happy with 19–21 °C water (swims are shorter but everything else still flies). Outside that window party charters still run but the on-deck temperature won't sustain a long anchor stop. TITLE: Boat Rental Marbella No License 2026: Self-Drive 5m / 15hp Boats
DESCRIPTION: License-free boat rules in Spain (5m / 15hp / 2 NM). Our skippered fleet starts at €749 — no license needed because we provide the captain. Full guide to no-license boating in Marbella.

Home › Boat Rental Marbella No License 2026: Self-Drive 5m / 15hp Boats Skip the licence question: our fleet ( Astondoa 40 , Azimut 39 ) is crewed by a licensed skipper, so you don't need any license at all. From €749 for 2 h , group capacity 9–11 guests. The page below covers the licence-free rules if you want to drive a smaller boat yourself. The short version: yes, you can rent a boat in Marbella with no licence, no certificate, and no formal sailing experience — as long as you stick to the Spanish rule set of 5 metres hull, 15 horsepower engine, 2 nautical miles from the coast, daylight only, and you're at least 18 years old. From €130 for 2 hours, this is the cheapest and most flexible way to get on the water on the Costa del Sol. The Spanish no-licence rules (2026) Spain's leisure boating regulation 875/2014 defines the licence-free category — known officially as navegación sin titulación — with the following limits: Hull length: 5 metres maximum for motor boats, 6 metres for sail-only boats. Engine power: 15 hp maximum (no exceptions — operators are checked). Distance from coast: 2 nautical miles (~3.7 km). Most coastal anchorages from Marbella sit well inside this. Time of day: daylight only. You must return before sunset. Captain age: 18 years or over with a valid ID. Alcohol: the same blood-alcohol limit as driving applies to the helm operator. For the broader licensing picture (and what you can charter if you have a foreign certificate), see our Spain boat licence rules guide. Where to pick up your no-licence boat Three Marbella locations operate licence-free fleets: Cabopino — biggest no-licence fleet on this stretch of coast. Easiest exit (the harbour mouth is wide and the immediate water is shallow and forgiving). Most affordable. Puerto Banús — fewer no-licence operators, slightly higher prices, but a great location if you want to anchor off Nikki Beach for the afternoon. Marbella Marina (Puerto Deportivo) — small no-licence fleet, central location, easy parking nearby. If you're staying further west, Estepona has additional licence-free operators that are 15–20% cheaper than Puerto Banús for the same boat. Skip the licence — book skippered If you want the simpler path: book our crewed fleet. Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 run €749 for 2 h with the skipper included — no licence, no exam, no on-the-dock briefing. The page below explains the official Spanish licence-free rules for context, but they only apply if you want to drive a small (under 5 m / 15 hp) self-drive boat yourself. What to expect — your first time at the helm Arrive 30 minutes before departure. You'll show ID and credit card, sign a one-page rental contract, and the operator walks you through: Throttle and gear shift. One lever — forward, neutral, reverse. Outboards on 15 hp boats are simple. Steering. Either a wheel or, on the smallest boats, a tiller. Anchor. How to drop it and how to retrieve it without trapping fingers. VHF radio. Channel 16 is emergency; you'll rarely need it within 2 NM. Coastal exclusion zones. The 200 m swimmer buoy zone along every Marbella beach — you cannot cross it under power. Refuel procedure. Where the fuel dock is, payment method. The whole briefing takes 10–15 minutes. Then you take the boat for a controlled lap of the marina with the operator watching from the dock, and after that you're on your own. Good itineraries for a 4-hour licence-free trip From Cabopino: head west 20 minutes to anchor off the Cabopino dune beach, swim and snorkel for an hour, push west another 15 minutes to Río Real, swim again, return to Cabopino with 30 minutes of buffer. Stays inside 2 NM the whole time. From Puerto Banús: head east 25 minutes to anchor off Nikki Beach (water is deeper here, anchor sets cleanly), swim, optional tender visit, return via the Golden Mile coastline so you see the famous waterfront mansions. Easy first-timer route. Is a no-licence boat right for you? Yes if: you're a group of 2–4, you want flexibility on timing, you'd rather drive yourself than be a passenger, and you're comfortable with light wind chop. No if: you're 6+ people (capacity is 4–5 max), you want speed (15 hp is slow), you want range (2 NM is tight), or you don't enjoy thinking about wind and tide. For larger groups, look at yacht charter Marbella with a skipper, or compare all options on the boat rental Marbella hub. Frequently asked questions Can I really rent a boat in Marbella without a licence? Yes. Spanish maritime law permits any adult over 18 to operate a boat up to 5 metres long with an engine up to 15 hp (or a sailboat up to 6 m), within 2 nautical miles of the coast, in daylight, with no licence and no formal training. You'll do a 10-minute on-the-dock briefing with the operator covering throttle, anchor and VHF radio, then you're on your way. What can a 15 hp boat actually do? A 5 m / 15 hp boat in Marbella cruises at 8–12 knots fully loaded with 4 adults, planes lightly when solo or with two. You'll comfortably reach the anchor spots off Marbella Marina, Río Verde or the Cabopino dunes within 20–30 minutes. Estepona and Puerto Banús-to-Cabopino in a day is realistic. How much is a no-license boat rental in Marbella? €130 for 2 hours in low season (October to May), €180 in high season (June to September). Full half-day (4 hours) is €220–€280. Full day (8 hours) is €380–€450. Most operators take a refundable security deposit of €200–€300. Is fuel included? Practice varies. About half of Marbella operators include fuel in the rental price; the other half operate on 'return with the tank as you found it'. Either way, a 15 hp engine burns very little — a 2-hour cruise typically uses €8–€15 of petrol. What happens if I damage the boat? Standard policy: the security deposit covers minor damage. For anything larger, every license-free rental in Spain comes with mandatory third-party liability insurance, which the operator carries. You're not personally liable beyond the deposit unless you've been reckless or drunk at the helm. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

It's also the practical centre of Marbella yacht charter: roughly 60% of bookable inventory on the Costa del Sol berths here. Boat rental from Puerto Banús starts at €250 for a 2-hour skippered trip. Why Puerto Banús dominates Marbella boat rental Three reasons it's the default departure port: Depth and berth size. Puerto Banús has 915 berths and the deepest harbour mouth on the Marbella stretch — the only marina locally that can routinely accommodate yachts over 30 metres. If you want a true luxury charter, you'll be here. Fleet concentration. Sixty per cent of charter inventory between Estepona and Cabopino is moored at Puerto Banús. Catamarans, sportfishers, party yachts and crewed superyachts cluster here. Marina infrastructure. Refuelling, fresh water, electrical hook-up, marine chandlers and ten dockside restaurants for pre-boarding lunch — Puerto Banús has all of it within a 5-minute walk. Puerto Banús vs Marbella Marina breaks down the trade-offs for non-Banús options. Where to meet the boat Puerto Banús is laid out as eight long parallel pontoons running south-east into the harbour, numbered 1 through 8 from west to east: Pier Typical fleet Walk from car park 1 Superyachts 30 m+, charter brokers 3 min 2–3 Luxury motor yachts 14–22 m 5 min 4–5 Motor yachts 10–14 m, catamarans 6 min 6 Sportfishers, sailing yachts 7 min 7 Day boats, licence-free, small sailboats 8 min 8 Working fleet, fuel dock 10 min Your charter will send you the exact pier and slip number 24 hours before. Show up 15 minutes early — Mediterranean charters leave on time, and missing the slot in summer often means losing the booking. Parking, food and pre-board essentials Parking: Aparcamiento Puerto Banús (underground) is the only sane option in July and August. €2/hour, €18 for the day. Free street parking exists west of the marina but never works mid-summer. Quick lunch before boarding: Antonio's Beach Club (tapas, walking distance), Picasso (Spanish, faster service), or grab a bocadillo from the SuperSol on Calle Ribera. Forgot something: Galerías El Corte Inglés (5-minute walk west) for sunscreen, swimwear, hats, electronics, and a basic pharmacy. Typical departures from Puerto Banús Westbound: head past Estepona, anchor at Cala del Faro for a swim, optional lunch at Sotogrande, return as the sun sets behind Gibraltar. Eastbound: motor along the Golden Mile, swim off Río Verde, push on to Nikki Beach for a tender-in lunch, back via the central Marbella coastline. Sunset 2-hour: a slow westbound cruise to Río Verde and back, perfect for couples — full details on the dedicated sunset cruise Marbella page. How busy does Puerto Banús get? July and August Saturdays: extremely busy. The harbour mouth is single-file traffic with 80–100 boats leaving between 11:00 and 12:00, and the same crowd returning between 17:00 and 19:00. Skippers manage this competently but you'll wait 10–15 minutes at the exit. May, June and September are calmer; weekdays are calm all summer. October through April the marina is half-empty. Summary in one line Cancel 7+ days before = full refund. Skipper-cancelled for weather = full refund, always. Full schedule When you cancel Refund Notes 7+ days before charter 100% Full refund, no questions. 3–6 days before 50% The other 50% covers skipper hold + fuel sourcing. 48 hours – 3 days 25% Higher because we've usually turned other bookings down. Inside 48 hours 0% Booked period is committed. Day-of no-show 0% Skipper waited at the dock. Weather cancellations If the skipper cancels the charter because of wind, sea state or thunderstorm risk, you always get a 100% refund or a free reschedule to a date of your choice. The call is made the evening before based on Windguru / AEMET forecasts. Light rain alone is not a cancellation reason on the Costa del Sol — drizzle clears within minutes, the sea is usually flat. How to cancel Message Andra on WhatsApp with your booking date. Refunds are processed back to the original payment method within 3–7 business days. Rescheduling vs cancelling Rescheduling is always free if we can find a new date in the same season. If the new date is in a different season (e.g. December → August), the new-season price applies — we'll show you the difference before you confirm. Add-ons and catering Add-ons booked separately (catered lunch, photographer, custom decoration) follow the catering provider's own cancellation terms — we forward 100% of what we collect, no markup. We'll always show you those terms when you add the line item. Force majeure If the charter is impossible because of events outside our control (port closure, government health order, harbour-wide emergency), you get a 100% refund regardless of how close to the date. Travel insurance We recommend buying travel insurance with cruise / charter cover for any booking over €1,500. Standard travel insurance often excludes private yacht charter — read your policy. TITLE: Catamaran Rental Marbella 2026: Sail &amp; Power Catamarans from €350
DESCRIPTION: Looking for catamaran rental in Marbella? Our current fleet is two 12.5 m flybridge motor yachts from Puerto Banús (€749/2h). Plus a full guide to catamaran charter options in Marbella.

Home › Catamaran Rental Marbella 2026: Sail & Power Catamarans from €350 Our fleet right now: two 12.5 m flybridge motor yachts — the Astondoa 40 (9 guests) and Azimut 39 (11 guests). From €749 for 2h , up to €2,299 for a full 8-hour day . Skipper, fuel, drinks, insurance and VAT included. Catamaran rental in Marbella means a stable twin-hull platform, far more deck space than a monohull yacht of the same length, and a flat-water ride that keeps even seasickness-prone guests relaxed. Prices start at €350 for 4 hours on a 38-ft sail catamaran from Puerto Banús and scale up to €2,400 for a full day on a 50-ft luxury catamaran. Why a catamaran beats a monohull in Marbella Three concrete advantages for the typical Marbella day charter: Stability. The Costa del Sol gets a 12–18 knot easterly (the levante) most summer afternoons. A monohull yacht heels 15–20°; a catamaran heels 2–3°. Glasses stay on tables, kids stay calm, and lunch stays on the plate. Deck space. A 40 ft catamaran has the deck area of roughly a 55 ft monohull. Bridgedeck cockpit, two helm stations, forward trampolines for sun-lounging, and a swim platform across the full stern. Shallow draft. Catamarans typically draw 1.1–1.3 metres versus 2.0–2.5 metres for a monohull. The skipper can anchor closer to shore at Cala del Faro, Cabopino dunes or Río Real — which puts the swim ladder over clearer, calmer water. Catamaran rental Marbella prices 2026 Catamaran 4 h low 4 h high Full day 8 h Day guests 38–42 ft sail catamaran €350 €420 €1,000–€1,250 10–12 44–48 ft sail catamaran €450 €580 €1,400–€1,700 12 40–45 ft power catamaran €520 €680 €1,600–€1,900 10–12 50 ft luxury catamaran €700 €900 €1,800–€2,400 12–14 Prices include skipper, fuel and standard drinks. For comparison with motor yachts of similar length, see yacht charter Marbella . Sail vs power catamaran Sail catamarans are the romantic, atmospheric option. When the wind cooperates (most afternoons May–October), the skipper unfurls the genoa and you sail in near silence at 6–8 knots. The downside: under engine alone, sail catamarans only do 7–9 knots, so reaching Sotogrande and back in 4 hours isn't realistic. Power catamarans cruise at 18–22 knots with twin diesels, which makes long itineraries viable, but you trade the quiet sail experience for engine noise. For pure swim-and-lunch days where range doesn't matter, the sail catamaran is almost always the better experience. For Gibraltar trips or anywhere you want to cover ground, the power cat or a motor yacht makes more sense. Catamaran itineraries from Puerto Banús The 4-hour catamaran sweet spot from Puerto Banús: depart 11:00, motor west 30 minutes to clear the marina traffic, raise sails off Río Verde, sail the Golden Mile coastline downwind, drop sails and anchor at Río Real for a long swim, motor back to Puerto Banús by 15:00 in time for a beach club afternoon. For a full day, add a second anchor stop at Cala del Faro past Estepona — total round trip about 35 NM. If you have kids on board , the flat catamaran deck is dramatically safer than a heeling monohull. What's included Standard catamaran charter inclusions in Marbella are identical to motor yacht charters: skipper, fuel, soft drinks, water, ice, light snacks, snorkel gear and an inflatable SUP or donut. Many catamaran operators also throw in fishing rods (light tackle for tuna or mackerel) and the use of the on-board BBQ at anchor — ask when booking. Best months for catamaran rental in Marbella May, June, September and October are ideal — steady 8–14 knot wind for proper sailing, sea temperature 19–23 °C, and 10–15% lower prices than peak July/August. July and August deliver hotter weather but variable wind: sometimes glassy mornings (motor only) and stronger afternoons. November to March, sail catamaran trips still run on calm days but choice of operators is more limited. How to book Tell us your date, group size, sail vs power preference and budget. We'll send back specific catamaran options with photos and exact pricing. Confirm with 50% deposit; balance on the boarding day. The full boat rental Marbella hub compares catamarans against every other boat type if you're still deciding. Frequently asked questions Why pick a catamaran over a monohull yacht? Two reasons: stability and deck space. A catamaran sits flat on the water with two hulls — even in 15-knot wind there's almost no heel — so guests who get queasy on monohulls can usually relax. The bridgedeck and forward trampolines also give you 40–50% more usable deck space at the same overall length, which matters when you have 10+ guests. How much is catamaran rental in Marbella? Catamaran rental in Marbella starts at €350 for 4 hours on a 38-ft sail catamaran in low season, €420 in high season. An 8-hour day on the same catamaran is €1,000–€1,250 fully crewed. Larger 50-ft catamarans run €600 for 4 hours and €1,800–€2,400 for a day. Sail catamaran or power catamaran? Sail catamarans give you the option to actually sail when the wind is right (typically a Levante easterly mid-afternoon) — it's quieter and more romantic, but slower. Power catamarans (twin-engine, no rig) deliver 18–22 knot cruising, which matters if you want to reach Sotogrande or Cabopino on the same day. For lunch-and-swim half-days, sail catamarans win on atmosphere. How many guests fit on a catamaran? Day-charter capacity on the standard 38–42 ft catamarans in Marbella is 10–12 guests. Larger 50-ft catamarans take 14–16 day guests. Overnight capacity is lower — typically 6–8 in cabins. For groups above 16, look at boat party options on motor yachts. Are catamarans good for snorkelling? Excellent. The shallow draft (often 1.2 m vs 2.5 m for a monohull) lets the skipper anchor closer to shore, which usually means clearer water for snorkelling. The wide swim platforms at the stern make getting in and out far easier than climbing down a monohull ladder. ★★★★★ 4.9 · verified reviews From €749 2h skippered charter Direct quotes from our own Puerto Banús fleet — same boats, same skippers, no third-party hand-off. 💬 Message on WhatsApp Avg reply < 5 min · No deposit until you confirm 📅 Book now Browse the full fleet → Compare live prices on BoatHire24 → All charters include Licensed skipper & fuel Drinks: water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks Insurance & safety equipment Snorkel gear & inflatables VAT 21% — no surprises

TITLE: Bootsführerschein in Spanien: Regeln 2026 für Marbella einfach erklärt
DESCRIPTION: Bootsführerschein in Spanien — wann du keinen brauchst, welche deutschen Scheine gelten und wie führerscheinfreies Fahren in Marbella funktioniert (5 m / 15 PS).

Bootsverleih › Bootsführerschein Spanien Die häufigste Frage deutscher Gäste: „Brauche ich für ein Boot in Marbella einen Führerschein?“ Die kurze Antwort: oft nicht. Hier sind die Regeln für 2026 klar erklärt. Wann du KEINEN Führerschein brauchst Mit Skipper — bei ~95% unserer Charters fährt ein lizenzierter Skipper. Du brauchst gar nichts und kannst dich entspannen. Führerscheinfrei selbst fahren — erlaubt bei Booten bis 5 m Rumpflänge und max. 15 PS , innerhalb 2 Seemeilen von der Küste, nur bei Tageslicht, Fahrer mindestens 18 Jahre. Siehe unsere Boote ohne Führerschein ab €230/2h. Welche deutschen Scheine in Spanien gelten Wer ein größeres Boot selbst steuern will, braucht eine anerkannte Lizenz. In der Praxis werden gängige internationale Scheine akzeptiert: SBF-See (Sportbootführerschein See) — für küstennahe Fahrten meist ausreichend. SKS (Sportküstenschifferschein) — für größere/weitere Charter. ICC (International Certificate of Competence) — international anerkannt. Bring immer das Original-Zertifikat (kein Foto) mit. Im Zweifel schick uns vorab ein Bild auf WhatsApp — wir sagen dir, ob es für das gewünschte Boot reicht. Die führerscheinfreien Grenzen im Detail Regel Grenze Bootslänge max. 5 m Motorleistung max. 15 PS Abstand zur Küste max. 2 Seemeilen Tageszeit nur bei Tageslicht Mindestalter Fahrer 18 Jahre Unser Tipp Die meisten Gäste buchen einfach mit Skipper — kein Papierkram, kein Stress, und ihr lernt die besten Buchten von einem Local. Wer den Nervenkitzel selbst fahren will, nimmt ein führerscheinfreies Boot oder eine geführte Jetski-Tour . ★★★★★ 4.9 · verifizierte Bewertungen Ab €749 2h Charter mit Skipper WhatsApp jetzt — durchschnittliche Antwort in unter 5 Minuten. Sobre los patrones de nuestra flota Todos nuestros patrones tienen titulación oficial española (PER — Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo, o superior) y al menos 5 años de experiencia en la Costa del Sol. Conocen cada fondeo, cada chiringuito accesible por mar, las horas óptimas para evitar el viento de levante, y cómo maniobrar Puerto Banús un sábado de agosto. Hablan español, inglés y la mayoría también francés o italiano. Dejarán que conduzcas un rato en tramos seguros si te apetece probar el timón. Reglamentación española al detalle La normativa de embarcaciones sin titulación viene del Real Decreto 875/2014. Los límites simultáneos son: Eslora máxima: 5 m (motor) / 6 m (vela) Potencia máxima: 15 cv Distancia máxima de la costa: 2 millas náuticas (~3,7 km) Solo horario diurno, sin pernoctación Conductor con al menos 18 años cumplidos Misma normativa de alcohol que para conducción terrestre El Servicio Marítimo de la Guardia Civil hace patrullas aleatorias en julio-agosto, especialmente entre Puerto Banús y Cabopino. Si te paran y el motor supera los 15 cv en una embarcación supuestamente "sin titulación", la multa al alquilador es de €600+ y al conductor le retiran el timón en el momento. Por eso los operadores serios son estrictos con el límite. Preguntas frecuentes ¿De verdad puedo alquilar un barco sin licencia en Marbella? Sí — con dos opciones. Reserva un yate con patrón profesional (nuestra flota, desde €749/2h) y eres pasajero, sin ningún requisito de licencia. O alquila una embarcación pequeña (hasta 5 m / 15 cv) en operadores especializados, sin titulación pero con limitaciones (2 NM de costa, sólo de día). ¿Hace falta saber navegar? No con nuestros barcos — el patrón conduce. Sólo te sientas, disfrutas y das instrucciones del tipo "un baño más antes de volver". ¿Cuántos puedo llevar? Astondoa 40 hasta 9 invitados, Azimut 39 hasta 11, Mangusta 80 hasta 12. ¿Puedo conducir un rato? Sí, durante tramos seguros y bajo supervisión del patrón. Es una práctica habitual y los patrones suelen estar encantados de que pruebes el timón. Con nuestros clientes a bordo Charters reales con grupos reales en Marbella, Puerto Banús y la Costa del Sol. TITLE: Política de Privacidad — Alquiler de Barcos Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Política de privacidad de boatrentalinmarbella.com — qué datos recopilamos cuando nos contactas por WhatsApp, email o formulario, cómo los usamos y tus derechos RGPD.

Política de Privacidad Última actualización: 1 de junio de 2026 Esta política explica cómo Boat Rental Marbella (sitio web boatrentalinmarbella.com , en adelante "nosotros") recopila, utiliza y protege tus datos personales cuando visitas el Sitio, nos contactas por WhatsApp, email o el formulario de reserva, o contratas un chárter. Cumplimos con el Reglamento (UE) 2016/679 (RGPD) y la LOPDGDD 3/2018. 1. Responsable del tratamiento Boat Rental Marbella Marbella, Andalucía, España (29602) WhatsApp / teléfono: +358 400 406 194 2. Datos que recopilamos Reservas y consultas — nombre, teléfono, email, fechas de interés, número de personas, puerto de salida, idioma, y el texto libre que envíes. Pagos — para reservas confirmadas el pago lo procesa el operador del barco (normalmente Stripe, Revolut Business o transferencia). No guardamos números de tarjeta completos. Datos técnicos — dirección IP, navegador, dispositivo, página de origen, páginas vistas y marcas de tiempo. Recogidos por los logs del servidor y Google Analytics 4 (ID G-SPMRYJVTHD). Cookies — ver apartado 7. 3. Para qué los usamos (base jurídica) Atender tu consulta y organizar el chárter — base: ejecución de un contrato o medidas pre-contractuales (art. 6.1.b RGPD). Compartir la reserva con el operador / patrón — base: contrato (art. 6.1.b). Cumplir con la normativa fiscal y marítima española (facturación, lista de pasajeros si el capitán la requiere) — base: obligación legal (art. 6.1.c). Mejorar el Sitio y medir tráfico — base: interés legítimo (art. 6.1.f). Comunicaciones comerciales — solo con tu consentimiento expreso (art. 6.1.a), revocable en cualquier momento. Actualmente no enviamos newsletter. 4. Destinatarios Operador / patrón asignado al chárter (nombre, teléfono, fecha, número de personas). Procesadores de pago (Stripe, Revolut, etc.). Proveedores de comunicación — WhatsApp (Meta Platforms Ireland Ltd.), Gmail / Google Workspace, Instagram y Facebook (Meta). Alojamiento y analítica — GitHub Pages (Microsoft Corp., EE. UU.), Cloudflare, Google Analytics 4 (Google Ireland Ltd.). Hacienda, aseguradoras y autoridades cuando exista obligación legal. Algunos proveedores están en EE. UU. Las transferencias se basan en el Marco de Privacidad de Datos UE–EE. UU. o en las Cláusulas Contractuales Tipo de la Comisión Europea. 5. Conservación Consultas sin reserva — eliminadas en 12 meses. Reservas y facturas — 6 años (obligación fiscal española). Logs y analítica — 14 meses (por defecto en Google Analytics). Registros de consentimiento — hasta su retirada más un periodo razonable de archivo. 6. Tus derechos Puedes ejercer en cualquier momento los derechos de acceso, rectificación, supresión, limitación, oposición, portabilidad y retirada del consentimiento, enviándonos un WhatsApp al +358 400 406 194 con copia de un documento de identidad. Responderemos en un máximo de 30 días. También puedes reclamar ante la Agencia Española de Protección de Datos (AEPD) . 7. Cookies Estrictamente necesarias — para mostrar el Sitio, recordar el idioma y el borrador del formulario. No requieren consentimiento. Analítica — Google Analytics 4 ( _ga , _ga_* ) de forma anónima. Puedes desactivarlas con el complemento de inhabilitación de Google Analytics . Embeds externos — solo activan cookies si haces clic en enlaces a Instagram, Facebook o WhatsApp. 8. Menores El Sitio no se dirige a menores de 14 años y no recopilamos conscientemente sus datos. Los menores pueden viajar a bordo del chárter — sus datos los facilita el padre, madre o tutor legal en la reserva. 9. Seguridad El Sitio funciona sobre HTTPS. Los mensajes por WhatsApp están cifrados de extremo a extremo por Meta. Los registros internos se guardan en servicios cloud (Google Workspace) con acceso controlado y verificación en dos pasos. 10. Cambios Podemos actualizar esta política cuando sea necesario; la fecha de "Última actualización" arriba indica la versión vigente. Los cambios sustanciales se anunciarán en la portada durante al menos 30 días. 11. Contacto Cualquier consulta de privacidad: WhatsApp +358 400 406 194 . TITLE: Términos y Condiciones — Alquiler de Barcos Marbella
DESCRIPTION: Términos y condiciones de los alquileres de barcos reservados a través de boatrentalinmarbella.com — reserva, precios, pago, cancelación, meteorología, seguridad, responsabilidad y ley aplicable.

Términos y Condiciones Última actualización: 1 de junio de 2026 Estos términos regulan cualquier reserva, chárter, consulta o uso del sitio boatrentalinmarbella.com ("el Sitio"), operado por Boat Rental Marbella ("nosotros"). Al enviar una consulta, aceptar un presupuesto, abonar una señal o embarcar en un barco contratado, confirmas que has leído, entendido y aceptado estos términos. 1. Sobre nosotros Boat Rental Marbella es una agencia independiente de chárter con base en Marbella, Andalucía, España. Coordinamos chárteres con patrón de yates, catamaranes, lanchas y motos de agua que operan desde Puerto Banús, Puerto Deportivo de Marbella (Virgen del Carmen), Cabopino, Estepona y Sotogrande. Los chárteres son operados por patrones con licencia española y empresas colaboradoras con los permisos marítimos y comerciales correspondientes. WhatsApp / teléfono: +358 400 406 194 2. Proceso de reserva Nos envías una consulta a través del formulario, WhatsApp, email o DM de Instagram/Facebook, indicando fecha, número de personas y barco o experiencia preferida. Respondemos con disponibilidad y presupuesto por escrito (normalmente en pocas horas, a menudo en minutos durante el horario europeo). La reserva queda confirmada solo cuando (a) enviamos confirmación por escrito y (b) se recibe la señal o el pago total acordados. Te enviamos la dirección de salida, contacto del patrón, hora de quedada y briefing por WhatsApp al menos 24 horas antes del chárter. 3. Precios, pago y qué incluye Los precios se indican en euros (€), incluyen el IVA español (actualmente 21%) y — salvo indicación expresa — incluyen patrón con licencia, combustible para la ruta costera estándar, refrescos y agua, equipo de snorkel, paddleboard, toallas y seguro estándar. Lo siguiente normalmente no está incluido salvo mención: catering, bebidas premium, combustible de juguetes acuáticos (p. ej. salida en moto de agua fuera del chárter), tasas de atraque en puertos de terceros, propinas y extras opcionales (DJ, fotógrafo, decoración). Las formas de pago varían según el operador — habitualmente una señal del 30–50% por tarjeta, enlace Stripe, Revolut o transferencia SEPA para confirmar, y el resto en efectivo o tarjeta el día del chárter. Los términos concretos figuran en el presupuesto escrito. 4. Política de cancelación, modificación y reembolso Salvo que tu confirmación indique algo distinto, las condiciones estándar son: 7 o más días antes de la salida — reembolso del 100% de lo abonado. Entre 3 y 6 días antes — reembolso del 50%. Menos de 48 horas antes — sin reembolso. No presentarse — sin reembolso. Los cambios de fecha son gratuitos si se solicitan con al menos 72 horas de antelación y dependen de disponibilidad. 5. Política meteorológica El patrón es la única autoridad para decidir si la salida puede realizarse con seguridad. Si la previsión de viento supera la fuerza 4–5 (~20+ nudos), el estado de la mar es inseguro, o la autoridad portuaria emite una advertencia, el patrón aplazará el chárter — habitualmente la tarde anterior o antes de las 08:00 del mismo día. En ese caso te ofrecemos: una nueva fecha sin coste adicional (sujeto a disponibilidad), o el reembolso íntegro de lo abonado. La lluvia ligera por sí sola no es motivo de cancelación en la Costa del Sol. Si cancelas por motivos meteorológicos cuando el patrón considera la salida segura, aplican las condiciones del apartado 4. 6. Seguridad, conducta y autoridad del patrón A bordo, el capitán tiene plena autoridad legal y operativa sobre el barco y los pasajeros conforme a la legislación marítima española. El capitán puede, a su entera discreción y sin reembolso: denegar el embarque a personas visiblemente ebrias, bajo efectos de drogas o con conducta agresiva; alterar la ruta, acortar el chárter o regresar a puerto por motivos de seguridad; obligar al uso de chaleco salvavidas; prohibir el baño, los saltos desde el barco o el uso de juguetes acuáticos cuando las condiciones no sean seguras. Todos los pasajeros deben seguir las instrucciones del capitán y el briefing de seguridad. Los menores de 12 años deben llevar chaleco siempre que el barco esté en navegación. Las mascotas solo se aceptan si se ha acordado previamente y deben llevar un chaleco específico para perros aportado por el propietario. 7. Conducta, daños y limpieza Las drogas de cualquier tipo están terminantemente prohibidas a bordo — el capitán está obligado por ley a regresar a puerto y avisar a la Guardia Civil. El cliente es responsable de cualquier daño al barco, equipamiento, juguetes acuáticos o bienes de terceros causados por él o sus invitados por negligencia o dolo, incluyendo cristalería rota, tapicería manchada, equipo de snorkel perdido o dañado y limpieza extraordinaria (p. ej. vómitos, derrames de comida que requieran limpieza profunda — entre 150 € y 500 €). 8. Retrasos y prolongaciones El chárter comienza a la hora acordada. Si llegas tarde, el chárter finaliza igualmente a la hora prevista, sin extensión ni reembolso. Prolongar el chárter el mismo día suele ser posible si el siguiente turno está libre — el tiempo adicional se cobra al 80–100% de la tarifa horaria y debe acordarse con el patrón antes de finalizar el turno reservado. 9. Seguro Todo barco contratado cuenta con el seguro de casco y responsabilidad civil obligatorio según la legislación española. No cubre objetos personales (móviles, cámaras, joyas, gafas de sol), condiciones médicas preexistentes ni pérdidas causadas por el incumplimiento de las normas de seguridad. Recomendamos contratar un seguro de viaje con cobertura de chárter, especialmente en reservas de alto valor o grupos numerosos. 10. Fotografía y contenido Ocasionalmente el patrón o un operador de dron pueden tomar fotos o vídeos cortos durante el chárter. Al embarcar consientes el uso no identificativo de este material en nuestra web y redes sociales (Instagram, Facebook, TikTok, YouTube). Si no quieres aparecer, comunícaselo al patrón antes de zarpar y te excluiremos. Puedes retirar el consentimiento sobre imágenes ya publicadas escribiéndonos por WhatsApp al +358 400 406 194 . 11. Responsabilidad En la máxima medida permitida por la ley española, nuestra responsabilidad por cualquier pérdida derivada de tu chárter se limita al importe total pagado por dicho chárter. No respondemos por daños indirectos o consecuenciales (vuelos perdidos, alojamiento, pérdida de disfrute) causados por cancelaciones meteorológicas, fuerza mayor (temporales, órdenes gubernamentales, huelgas, pandemias, cierres portuarios) o actos de terceros. Nada en estos términos excluye la responsabilidad por fallecimiento o lesiones personales causadas por negligencia, ni cualquier otra responsabilidad que no pueda excluirse según la normativa española de consumo. 12. Divulgación de afiliación Algunos enlaces del Sitio apuntan a operadores o plataformas de reserva de terceros. Cuando recomendamos un operador podemos recibir una comisión si reservas a través nuestro. Esto nunca afecta al precio que pagas y solo destacamos operadores que nosotros mismos contrataríamos. 13. Propiedad intelectual Todo el texto, fotos, vídeos y gráficos del Sitio son © Boat Rental Marbella o se usan bajo licencia. Puedes compartir páginas concretas en redes sociales y enlazar al Sitio libremente. No puedes republicar, raspar (scraping) ni usar el contenido para entrenar modelos de IA sin nuestro permiso escrito. Los nombres de marcas de barcos (Astondoa, Azimut, Mangusta, Ferretti, etc.) pertenecen a sus respectivos fabricantes y se utilizan solo a efectos descriptivos. 14. Ley aplicable y jurisdicción Estos términos se rigen por la legislación española. Cualquier conflicto con un consumidor se resolverá ante los tribunales del lugar de residencia del consumidor dentro de la UE, o ante los tribunales de Marbella (Málaga) para clientes fuera de la UE. Los consumidores también pueden usar la plataforma europea de resolución de litigios en línea en ec.europa.eu/consumers/od

TITLE: Estepona Boat Charter in October: Shoulder Season Seas
DESCRIPTION: Estepona boat charter weather October: 21°C seas, 8h sun, calm Med. Book a skipper-led charter from Puerto Banús. Check availability now.

Home › Estepona Boat Charter in October: Shoulder Season Seas October in Estepona is a hidden gem for boaters: the summer heat fades, but the Mediterranean stays warm at 21°C, and the sea is often glassy calm. With 8 hours of daily sunshine and air temperatures peaking at 24°C, it's the perfect shoulder-season window for a leisurely charter along the Costa del Sol. Our fleet, departing from Puerto Banús, is fully equipped with licensed skippers, fuel, drinks, and snacks—so you can focus on the views. Why October Works for an Estepona Boat Charter October avoids the two extremes: the crowded, hot summer and the cooler, windier winter. The sea temperature stays at a comfortable 21°C—still warm enough for a dip—and the typical wind is just 8-12 knots from the east, keeping the swell below 0.5m. You'll have the anchorages near Estepona, Cala del Faro, and Cala Cortés largely to yourself, with clear visibility for spotting dolphins along the coast. Estepona Boat Charter Weather October: What to Expect Metric October Average Air temperature (high) 24°C Air temperature (low) 17°C Sea temperature 21°C Sunshine 8 hours/day Rain probability 20% (brief showers) Wind speed 8-12 knots (east) Sea state Calm (1-2 Beaufort) These conditions are ideal for a stable, comfortable ride. The mornings are crisp, the afternoons warm, and the light is golden—perfect for photography off the Golden Mile. Fishing boat rental in Marbella covers two distinct experiences: a quiet 2-hour inshore trip pulling sea bream and mackerel from 20-metre reefs off the Golden Mile, or a 4–8 hour deep-sea expedition chasing dorado and tuna 8–15 nautical miles offshore over deeper seamounts. Fishing on our motor yachts Our 12.5 m fleet ( Astondoa 40 , Azimut 39 ) can run light tackle inshore fishing and trolling on request — same pricing as any other charter, €749 for 2 h up to €2,299 for a full 8-hour day . What you can catch off Marbella, by season Dorado (mahi-mahi) — June to October. Schooling fish, electric strikes on trolled feathers, 4–12 kg average. The headline summer target. Bluefin tuna — May to July. Heavy tackle, stand-up gear, fish ranging 50–150 kg. Strict ICCAT quota: most Marbella charters are catch-and-release for recreational anglers. Amberjack — year-round, peak winter. Jigging or live bait over deep reefs at 30–80 m. Strong fighters, 5–25 kg. Bonito and Atlantic bonito — September to November. Easy fast action, great for kids. Inshore: sea bream, sea bass, mackerel, barracuda — year-round, light tackle, ideal beginner targets. Inshore vs deep-sea — which to book? Inshore (2–4 hours): stay within 2 NM of the coast, fish reefs and wrecks at 15–40 m depth, calm water, lighter tackle, lower price. Best for families, kids, first-timers, anyone who gets queasy. Deep-sea (4–8 hours): run 8–15 NM offshore to seamounts and current edges, troll lures or drift live bait, target bigger species, longer travel time, more boat motion. Best for serious anglers and groups happy to commit a full day. If you're undecided, the half-day inshore is the safer first booking — you can always upgrade next trip. For everything we offer across the fleet, the boat rental Marbella hub compares fishing alongside yachts and catamarans. Departure ports Puerto Banús hosts the biggest deep-sea sportfishers (10–12 m Bertram-class). Cabopino is the cheapest pickup for inshore trips and the closest harbour to the productive eastern reefs around Río Real. La Bajadilla fishing port (next to Marbella Marina) keeps the working-fleet feel and is where local skippers with 30+ years on the water are based. Estepona works for west-bound offshore tuna trips when current is favourable. What's included on every fishing charter Licensed skipper plus a mate (deep-sea) or just skipper (small inshore) Rods, reels and complete terminal tackle (hooks, leaders, swivels) Live bait (sardines, mackerel, squid) appropriate to target species Ice box for your catch Soft drinks and water Filleting and bagging of keepers at the dock Fishing licence coverage under the boat's commercial permit Not included: lunch (BYO sandwich or order a catered lunch box), alcohol (most skippers ask you to wait until you're back at the dock), and trip insurance for offshore days. From May to October the headliner is dorado (mahi-mahi) — schooling fish, fast bites, great for first-time anglers. Bluefin tuna season is May–July; expect 50–150 kg fish on heavy tackle. Amberjack are reliable year-round at 30–80 m depth. Bonito and Atlantic bonito show up September–November. Inshore trips target sea bream, mackerel, sea bass and small barracuda. Do I need a fishing licence? Yes — Andalucía requires a Licencia de Pesca Marítima de Recreo for sport fishing. Reputable charters include the licence in the boat's commercial permit, so as a paying guest you're covered. You'll need ID on board (passport or EU card) and the skipper handles paperwork. Can I keep the fish? Yes, within the daily quota. Andalucía allows 5 kg per person per day for recreational sea fishing, plus one fish over the size limit. Bluefin tuna is catch-and-release only during open season unless your skipper holds a commercial bluefin quota. Is it suitable for beginners? Yes — the mate does the heavy lifting and walks you through reel mechanics in 5 minutes. Inshore trips are the easiest first experience: shorter run-out, calmer water, lighter tackle. What you can do on the water The Marbella coast is the perfect playground for a one-hour Sea-Doo session. The 200 m coastal swim-zone rule applies, but outside it you have miles of open water along the Golden Mile and east towards Cabopino. Typical 1-hour itineraries: Puerto Banús → Río Verde → back — quick tour past the marquee beach clubs (Nikki Beach, Ocean Club). The Sea-Doo experience Modern Sea-Doo PWC are easy to ride — twist-grip throttle, lanyard kill switch, intuitive handlebars. The 10-minute on-the-dock briefing covers everything you need to know. You will: Throttle and brake. Right thumb forward to go, release to coast. Sea-Doos have an iBR brake-and-reverse system that makes docking much easier than older jet skis. Kill switch lanyard. Clipped to your life jacket. If you fall off, the engine stops automatically. The 200 m rule. Stay outside the swim-buoy line along every Marbella beach. Penalties are stiff and the Guardia Civil del Mar patrol. Wake awareness. Slow inside the Puerto Banús harbour mouth and when passing other boats — keep your wake low. After the briefing you take the Sea-Doo out for a controlled lap of the marina with our staff watching, and from there you're on your own. TITLE: Luxury Yacht Rental Marbella 2026: Superyachts &amp; Crewed Charters
DESCRIPTION: Luxury yacht charter Marbella from €749 on our 12.5 m Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 motor yachts — licensed skipper, drinks and VAT included. Crewed charters from Puerto Banús.

Home › Luxury Yacht Rental Marbella 2026: Superyachts & Crewed Charters Our fleet right now: two 12.5 m flybridge motor yachts — the Astondoa 40 (9 guests) and Azimut 39 (11 guests). From €749 for 2h , up to €2,299 for a full 8-hour day . Skipper, fuel, drinks, insurance and VAT included. Luxury yacht rental in Marbella means stepping onto an 18-to-30-metre crewed yacht at Puerto Banús, being greeted by a stewardess with a chilled glass, and handing the day over to a professional crew while you and your guests do nothing but enjoy it. Day charters start at €2,400 and scale up to €18,000 for the largest superyachts. Weekly charters begin around €25,000 plus expenses. Where the line is Yacht charter in Marbella exists on a continuum, and "luxury" is the top end of it. Practical breakpoints: 10-15 m motor yacht: private day boat. Captain only. Comfortable but not luxury. Covered on the yacht charter Marbella page. 18-22 m crewed yacht: entry-level luxury. Captain + stewardess. Hospitality-fitted interior, real galley, fold-out swim platform, water toys included. This is where most luxury bookings sit. 24-30 m yacht: superyacht. Full crew (captain, mate, chef, stewardess). Multiple staterooms, chef-prepared meals, on-board tender. Day charter possible but most owners prefer multi-day. 30 m+ megayacht: charter market thins out fast. Usually weekly or longer, crew of 5-10, full-board. Our fleet — luxury-tier amenities at the 12.5 m size class We currently run two crewed 12.5 m flybridge motor yachts at €749 → €2,299 per charter — both with cream-leather interiors, AC saloon, two cabins, and the same skipper-fuel-drinks-insurance-VAT inclusion as larger luxury yachts. See Astondoa 40 and Azimut 39 for full specs. For 18 m+ proper superyachts (weekly charters, APA model, full crew with chef) we can source through partners — message us with dates and group size. Weekly charter and the APA model Multi-day and weekly charters on 24 m+ yachts use the global APA system. The base charter fee covers the yacht, captain and crew salaries. On top of that you pre-pay an Advance Provisioning Allowance (APA) of 25-30% which funds fuel, marina fees, food, drink and any extra crew expenses during the trip. Captain accounts for every euro and refunds the unused portion at the end. A 24 m yacht for a week in low season: ~€25,000 + ~€7,000 APA. Same yacht in August: ~€40,000 + €10,000 APA. The crew and what they actually do Captain: commands the yacht — navigation, anchoring, all decisions affecting safety. Holds at least a 200 GT commercial licence. Your single point of contact for the trip. Stewardess: guest service. Welcomes you on board, mixes drinks, plates the food, handles cabin turn-down on overnight charters, keeps the saloon and decks tidy. The stewardess role on a luxury yacht is the difference between "nice boat" and "luxury experience". Chef: from 24 m up. You fill in a preference sheet before the charter — favourite cuisines, dietary requirements, allergies — and the chef builds a tailored menu for the trip. Provisioning is done from local markets (Mercado de Puerto Banús, La Cañada). Deckhand / mate: handles lines, fenders, tender operation, water-toys deployment, and keeps the exterior immaculate. Often a deck cadet building sea time toward their captain qualification. Where you can go Day charters cover the Costa del Sol — Sotogrande in the west, Cabopino in the east, with the Golden Mile coast in between. A long day with an early start reaches Gibraltar by boat from Marbella (100 NM round trip, only viable on flat-sea days). Overnight luxury charters extend the cruising window along the Marbella coast and the Strait. Weekly bookings out of Marbella typically anchor the itinerary around Puerto Banús and the Golden Mile stops most guests want to see. The booking process WhatsApp inquiry — dates, group size, length (day/weekend/week), special requirements. Shortlist — we send 2-4 specific yachts with photos, plans, crew bios, day rates and any APA estimates. Hold — preferred yacht is held for 48 hours while you decide. Generally 18 metres and above with captain plus stewardess minimum, hospitality-fitted interior, and water toys. Above 24 m you're in superyacht territory with chef and multiple staterooms. What does 'crewed charter' actually include? Captain (navigation), stewardess (guest service), chef from 24 m up (custom menus), deckhand (lines, tender, water toys). TITLE: What to Bring on a Marbella Boat Rental: 2040 Checklist
DESCRIPTION: Packing for a Marbella boat rental? Our 2040 checklist covers sun protection, seasickness meds, and what NOT to bring. Start your charter prepared.

Home › What to Bring on a Marbella Boat Rental: 2040 Checklist Packing for a Marbella boat rental in 2040 is simpler than you think — as long as you follow a few rules. Sun, sea, and a relaxed vibe are guaranteed, but forgetting the right gear can cut your day short. This checklist covers what to bring, what to leave at home, and what’s already on board. Whether you’re boarding an Astondoa 40 at Puerto Banús or a Mangusta 80, these tips apply to every charter. Our fleet departs from Puerto Banús, Marbella Marina, and Cabopino. Every rental includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and 21% IVA. You don’t need to bring any of those. But here’s what you do need. Sun Protection: The Non-Negotiables Marbella averages 320 sunny days a year. UV levels are high from April to October. Even on cloudy days, you can burn. Pack: Sunscreen SPF 50+ — water-resistant, at least 200 ml per person for a full day. Lip balm with SPF — wind and salt dry out lips fast. Sunglasses with polarised lenses — reduce glare off the water. Wide-brimmed hat or cap — a baseball cap won’t protect your ears or neck. UV-protective long-sleeve shirt or rash guard — especially if you burn easily. Reapply sunscreen every two hours and after swimming. The reflection off the sea increases exposure by up to 50%. Clothing and Footwear Dress for 22–26 °C sea temperatures and 28–35 °C air temperatures in summer. Evenings on the water can drop 10 °C, so bring a light jacket or windbreaker. Footwear is critical: Non-slip boat shoes — white or light soles only. Black soles mark the deck. No high heels — they damage the deck and are dangerous on a moving boat. Flip-flops — fine for swimming stops, but not for walking on deck while underway. Swimwear — bring a towel (not provided) and a dry change of clothes. If you’re on a Mangusta 80 charter , the deck is larger and more stable, but the same shoe rules apply. Health and Comfort Essentials Seasickness medication — take 30–60 minutes before departure. Even calm days can have a light swell. Personal medication — antihistamines, painkillers, or any prescription drugs you need. Hydration — water is provided, but bring your own reusable bottle if you prefer. Small dry bag — for phone, keys, wallet, and any electronics. Motion sickness bands — non-medicated alternative for mild sensitivity. Our skippers carry a basic first-aid kit, but it’s not stocked with personal medications. What NOT to Bring Glass bottles or containers — strictly prohibited on all boats. Broken glass on deck is dangerous and hard to clean. Drones — only allowed with prior written permission from the skipper. Puerto Banús and nearby beaches have strict no-fly zones. Large coolers — we provide drinks and snacks. If you bring extra food, use a soft-sided cooler bag. Speakers with external subwoofers — the boat’s sound system is sufficient. Loud external speakers disturb other charters and marine life. Black-soled shoes — they leave permanent marks on fibreglass decks. Illegal substances — zero tolerance. The skipper will terminate the charter and alert authorities. Documents and Practical Items You must bring a valid passport or EU national ID. The skipper needs a passenger list for insurance and legal compliance. A photo on your phone is not accepted. Also useful: Credit card or cash — for tips (optional, but appreciated) or any extras not included. Phone with offline maps — signal can be weak in some coves. Waterproof phone case — salt spray and splashes are common. Small backpack or tote — to keep your belongings together. What’s Already on Board Every Marbella boat rental with our fleet includes: Licensed skipper and fuel Water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava Light snacks (chips, olives, nuts) Insurance and safety equipment (life jackets, fire extinguisher, flares) 21% Spanish IVA On bigger boats like the Azimut 58 Flybridge or Ferretti 94, you also get a full sound system, onboard toilet, and shaded flybridge. Jet skis are included free on the Mangusta 80 (minimum 4-hour charter). Packing by Boat Type Boat Capacity Extra Tip Astondoa 40 / Azimut 39 9–11 pax Limited shade; bring extra sun protection Mangusta 80 (white/grey) 12 pax Jet ski included; bring swimwear and towel Bandido / Red Tide 6–8 pax Smaller deck; pack light Ferretti 94 / Canados 86 12 pax Luxury interior; dress smart-casual Speedboat (7m) 6 pax No cabin; keep valuables in dry bag Frequently asked questions Do I need to bring my own drinks on a Marbella boat rental? No. Every charter includes water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, and cava. If you want spirits or a specific brand, you can bring your own — but no glass bottles. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter License Requirements: Do You Need One?
DESCRIPTION: Do you need a licence to charter a yacht in Marbella? Find out the Marbella yacht charter license requirements, plus boat specs, prices, and inclusions.

Home › Marbella Yacht Charter License Requirements: Do You Need One? Planning a yacht charter in Marbella and wondering about the Marbella yacht charter license requirements ? You don't need one. All our charters include a fully licensed skipper who takes care of everything—navigation, mooring, safety, and even the drinks. You just show up, relax, and enjoy the Mediterranean. Here's everything you need to know about licences, age limits, and what's included. No Licence Needed for Our Charters Every boat in our fleet departs from Puerto Banús with a professional skipper. That means zero paperwork, no exams, and no prior experience. Whether you're on a Boat Rental Marbella Astondoa 40 or a Mangusta 80, the skipper handles the helm. You simply sit back and take in the views of La Concha, the Golden Mile, and the Costa del Sol coastline. What About Bareboat Charters? If you want to rent a boat without a skipper (bareboat), Spanish law requires a recreational licence called the PER (Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo). This allows you to operate vessels up to 15m in length. However, we don't offer bareboat hire—all our charters are skippered. So for our fleet, a licence is never needed. Age and ID Requirements You must be at least 18 to book a charter. All passengers need a valid ID or passport, which the skipper checks at departure. There's no upper age limit, but everyone should be comfortable boarding and moving around the boat. Life jackets are provided for all sizes, including children. Our Fleet at a Glance We have 29 boats ranging from a 6.4m speedboat to a 29m Ferretti 94. All depart from Puerto Banús, Marbella. Here are the highlights: Boat Length Max Pax Price From Astondoa 40 12.5m 9 €749/2h Azimut 39 12.5m 11 €749/2h Mangusta 80 (White/Grey) 24m 12 €4,719/4h Ferretti 94 29m 12 On request For all other boats, pricing is on request via WhatsApp. Every charter includes fuel, a licensed skipper, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, and Spanish IVA (21%). Can You Drive the Boat Yourself? Yes, with the skipper's permission. If you're curious about handling a 12.5m Azimut or a 24m Mangusta, the skipper can let you take the helm in open water. This is a great way to get a feel for yacht handling without needing a licence. The skipper remains responsible for safety, so you can learn in a controlled environment. Local Destinations You Can Visit From Puerto Banús, you can cruise to Cala del Faro, Cala Cortés, or head west to Estepona and Sotogrande. The skipper knows the best spots for swimming, snorkelling, and lunch at a beach club. No licence required—just pick your destination and enjoy. International Licence Holders If you already hold an International Certificate of Competence (ICC), RYA, or USCG licence, it's not needed for our charters. But it's a nice backup. For bareboat hire elsewhere in Spain, you'll need to check with local authorities whether your licence is recognised. Frequently Asked Questions Do I need a licence to charter a yacht in Marbella? No, not for a skippered charter. All our boats come with a licensed skipper who handles navigation, mooring, and safety. You simply relax and enjoy the coast. If you want to rent a boat without a skipper (bareboat), you'll need a Spanish recreational licence (PER) or an equivalent international certificate. What is the PER licence and do I need it? The Patrón de Embarcaciones de Recreo (PER) is a Spanish qualification for operating recreational boats up to 15m in length. It requires theory and practical exams. For our charters, you don't need it—our skippers take care of everything. Only consider it if you plan to hire a bareboat in Spain. Can I drive the boat myself during a skippered charter? Yes, with the skipper's permission and supervision, you can take the helm in open water. The skipper remains responsible for safety and navigation. This is a great way to get a feel for handling a boat without needing a licence. What if I have a foreign boating licence? International licences (ICC, RYA, USCG) are not required for our skippered charters. However, for bareboat hire in Spain, you may need to exchange your licence for a Spanish one or obtain a temporary permit. Check with the local maritime authority. Is there an age limit for chartering a yacht in Marbella? You must be at least 18 years old to book a charter. There's no upper age limit, but all passengers must be physically able to board and move around the boat. Children are welcome, and life jackets are provided for all sizes. What documents do I need to bring on the day? Just a valid ID or passport for all passengers. No licence, no certificate. The skipper will check your ID at departure. We recommend bringing sunscreen, sunglasses, and a light jacket for the evening. Can I charter a boat without a skipper in Marbella? Yes, but only if you hold a valid Spanish recreational licence (PER or similar). Our fleet is available for skippered charters only—we don't offer bareboat hire. If you want to go solo, you'll need to approach a bareboat specialist with your licence. What happens if I have a licence but still want a skipper? No problem. You can still book a skippered charter and enjoy a stress-free day. Some experienced boaters prefer having a skipper to handle mooring and local knowledge. Just let us know when booking. TITLE: Marbella Yacht Charter vs Barcelona: Costs &amp; Experiences
DESCRIPTION: Marbella yacht charter vs Barcelona: compare costs, distances, and experiences. Find out why Puerto Banús beats the Catalan capital for luxury boating.

Home › Marbella Yacht Charter vs Barcelona: Costs & Experiences When planning a luxury yacht charter on Spain’s Mediterranean coast, two destinations dominate: Marbella and Barcelona. Both offer stunning coastlines, but the experience, cost, and logistics differ significantly. This guide compares Marbella yacht charter vs Barcelona across key factors — price, distance to open sea, weather, and inclusions — so you can choose the best option for your trip. Cost Comparison: Marbella vs Barcelona Marbella consistently offers better value for luxury charters. Our Astondoa 40 (12.5m, 9 passengers) starts at €749 for 2 hours, including skipper, fuel, drinks, snacks, and 21% VAT. Barcelona equivalents often begin at €900–€1,200 for the same duration, with additional port fees adding 15–20%. For longer trips, Marbella’s 8-hour rate of €2,299 on the same boat undercuts Barcelona by at least €500. The Mangusta 80 (24m, 12 passengers) in Marbella includes a free jet ski for €4,719/4h — a package rarely offered in Barcelona without extra charges. Distance to Open Sea: Time Matters Marbella’s Puerto Banús is a 5-minute cruise to the Mediterranean. Barcelona’s main marinas (Port Olímpic, Port Vell) require 30–45 minutes of navigating the harbour before you reach open water. That’s 1–1.5 hours lost on a 4-hour charter. In Marbella, you’re swimming, sunbathing, or anchoring at Cala del Faro within 15 minutes of departure. Weather and Sea Conditions Marbella averages 320 sunny days per year, with summer sea temperatures of 22°C. Barcelona has 250 sunny days and slightly warmer water (24°C) due to its northern location. However, Marbella’s consistent sunshine and calmer seas (protected by La Concha mountain) make it more reliable for day trips. Barcelona’s Tramontana winds can create choppy conditions, especially in spring and autumn. Fleet Variety and Sizes Our Marbella fleet ranges from the 6.4m Mariah SX21 (5 passengers) to the 29m Ferretti 94 (12 passengers). Most boats depart from Puerto Banús, and we offer options for every group size. Barcelona fleets are similar but often require booking weeks in advance for popular models. Marbella’s year-round demand means more availability, even in peak August. Inclusions: What You Get Every Marbella charter includes a licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, safety equipment, and 21% VAT. Barcelona charters frequently add surcharges for catering, drinks, and fuel. Our packages are all-inclusive — no hidden costs. The Mangusta 80 even throws in a jet ski for free. Why Marbella (Puerto Banús) Leads the Costa del Sol Puerto Banús is the undisputed hub for luxury boat rentals on the Costa del Sol. It's 60 km from Málaga Airport (45 minutes by car), offers 915 berths, and has over 20 restaurants and shops right on the waterfront. Estepona: Quieter Waters, Fewer Yachts Estepona Marina, 85 km from Málaga Airport, is a smaller, more relaxed alternative. Cabopino: Small, Family-Friendly, East of Marbella Cabopino Marina, 15 km east of Marbella, is a tiny port with about 200 berths and a sandy beach. Cabopino is a good stop for a lunch break — not a launch point for a luxury charter. What size of groups can each destination accommodate? Marbella’s fleet handles up to 12 guests on yachts like the Azimut 58 Flybridge or Ferretti 94. Malaga’s largest rentals top out at 10–12 guests on boats under 15m. Marbella also has smaller options like the Dubhe (5 pax) for intimate groups. Are food and drinks included in Marbella yacht charters? Yes. Every Marbella charter includes water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, and light snacks. Malaga rentals often charge extra for catering or require you to bring your own. How far is Malaga from Marbella for a day trip? Malaga is about 60 km east of Marbella—a 45-minute drive via the AP-7 motorway. If you’re staying in Marbella, chartering locally saves travel time and fuel costs. Home › Marbella Yacht Charter vs Nice: Which Mediterranean Destination Wins? When planning a luxury yacht charter in the Mediterranean, two destinations dominate the conversation: Marbella and Nice. Both offer glamour, sunshine, and world-class marinas, but which one truly delivers the best experience? In this head-to-head comparison of Marbella yacht charter vs Nice, we break down the numbers — from sea temperatures and costs to anchorages and local culture. Spoiler: Marbella wins on value, weather, and length of season. Weather and Sailing Season Marbella boasts 320 sunny days per year, with summer sea temperatures reaching a balmy 24°C. The season runs from April to October, and many charters operate year-round for calm-day trips. Nice averages 270 sunny days, and sea temperatures peak at 22°C in August. The prime sailing window is June through August, with spring and autumn often too cool for comfortable swimming. If you want more time on the water, Marbella is the clear choice. Cost Comparison: All-Inclusive vs Hidden Extras Our Marbella charters are all-inclusive: licensed skipper, fuel, water, soft drinks, beer, white wine, cava, light snacks, insurance, and 21% VAT. In Nice, a similar-sized yacht might list for €4,000 but exclude fuel (€500–1,000), VAT (20%), and often crew gratuity. The real cost can exceed €6,000. For transparent pricing, Marbella wins. Anchorages and Scenery Marbella's coastline offers diverse anchorages: Cala del Faro, Cala Cortés, the dunes of Cabopino, and the exclusive Estepona coast. The Sierra Blanca mountains and La Concha peak provide a dramatic backdrop. Nice's anchorages — such as Villefranche-sur-Mer and Cap Ferrat — are stunning but more exposed to the Mistral wind. Marbella's lee shore and sheltered bays offer calmer seas and more swimming spots. Marina Glamour: Puerto Banús vs Nice Port Puerto Banús is the epicentre of luxury on the Costa del Sol. With 915 berths, designer boutiques, and celebrity-spotting opportunities, it's a destination in itself. Nice's Port Lympia is larger (1,200 berths) but feels more like a working marina. For high-end dining and nightlife, the Golden Mile and Puerto Banús offer a concentration of Michelin-starred restaurants and VIP beach clubs that surpass Nice. Puerto Banús offers world-class beach clubs and nightlife. Nice's old town is charming with Nicoise cuisine, but the high-end dining scene is less concentrated. For a full luxury experience, Marbella delivers more variety. Getting There Málaga Airport is 40 minutes from Puerto Banús, with flights from most European cities.
